Where a task's text changes its block's, a **Changed at assembly** bullet
says what changed and why. Everything else under a task line is its block's
detail, as the block wrote it, and is part of the task.

**Citations** in lane 3's text are spec.md's: `spec:<n>` and `svc:<n>` are
lines of the change's two spec files on this branch, at `ad82c2f4`; `Y:<n>`,
`ops:<n>` and `clar:<n>` are lines at Y. Inside a block's detail, the
block's own citations stand: lanes 1 and 4 cite Y, and lane 5 cites W
(`W:<n>`, W being `bb08d080`), as its block's Keys define it. Lane 4 cites
lane 1's contract draft 4.2 and block v4.2, whose line numbers block v4.11
keeps; drafts 4.12 to 4.22 keep them to :637 and move each later line down one.

**Marks**, inside the blocks' text:

- `[J3]`: lane 2's v3f fixes a test name or prefix, and the `-k` word beside
  it is a guess until lane 2's block names it.
- `[X: hosting]`, `[X: logins]`, `[X: backups]`: the block's record that
  commit X settled that text. They are settled; the mark stays as the
  block's history.
- `(PC-n)`: a choice the design leaves to the plan, as research.md's Part 3
  takes it.
- `(Un, lane n's)`: lane 3's 007 call (#165 6724332510), plan.md's U table.

**Tests**: the spec requires them, and lane 2 writes them (lane 3's ruling).
In each phase a test task comes before the implementation task it checks,
and its tests fail before that implementation begins. Each implementation
task's `verify:` names a module that lane 2's block creates.

**Organization**: by phase, then by owning lane. 007 is split by file
ownership, so a user story spans lanes. Each task names its story.

## Format: `[ID] [P?] [Story] [Tier] Description — files: … — verify: …`

- **[ID]**: kept from its block: T1xx lane 1, T2xx lane 2, T3xx lane 3, T4xx
  lane 4, T5xx lane 5 (PC-9).
- **[P]**: the task can run in parallel with the others marked so in its
  phase. They touch different files, and depend on no incomplete task.
- **[Story]**: US1 to US10, as in spec.md.
- **[Tier]**: the protocol's "Build Tiers": `[S]` runs on `sonnet-xhigh`,
  `[O]` on `opus-xhigh`.
  - There is no `[H]`. The code leg's `AGENTS.md` at `e39395895` has no
    `## Governed invariants` section (plan.md's check), so every task is
    `[S]` at least.
  - A task is `[O]` when it touches `lanes-index`, `lanes-edit.sh`,
    `openRepoTools`, `.github/workflows/tests.yml` or `tests/fixtures/`
    (plan.md, "Governed invariants"; PC-16), makes a design choice, a
    contract, a schema or a boundary, changes two modules, or is
    security-sensitive. Otherwise it is `[S]`.
- **`files:`** lists every path the task may create, change or delete,
  relative to the code worktree, `worktrees/007-sql-register/code/`. A
  spec-leg path is written `../spec/<path>` (PC-8). A diff outside the list
  fails the task.
- **`verify:`** runs from the code worktree and exits 0 only when the task is
  done. It takes R28's anchored two-run form: lane 2's module as a file
  argument with a `-k` that names it, then a regression run, each with
  `TMPDIR=/tmp`.
  - Never wrap a verify in `flock`: `tests/run.sh` locks itself. Run it from
    a shell with no `LANES_*` or `TMUX` inherited from a lane session. A
    driver that runs many verifies pauses between them.
  - `OPENREPOTOOLS_REQUIRE_PG=1` makes a missing engine pytest's exit 4, so
    a real-engine test can never pass by skipping.
- **Each task line** is followed by:
  - **Source:** its block line;
  - **Depends on:** the tasks it waits for, with "lane 2's T2xx: <item>"
    where lane 2's block names the task, as "Lane 2's block" below says, and
    `PENDING-L2: <item>` where it does not yet; `SPEC-PR` on every task but those
    whose output is already in this spec branch, T101, T102 and T344, which
    ride the spec PR; and `SLICE-1A` or `SLICE-1B` for the gates below.

## Gates and order

- **The spec PR first.** No 007 code PR lands before the spec PR
  (6726556972). Every Phase 0 PR lands after it too, the root switch R1
  included: Y lists the change's PR first and the root `composed.yml` PR
  after it (ops:603–605). Coding may start now. Every task's **Depends on**
  names `SPEC-PR`, but T344, whose whole output is already in this spec
  branch, rides the spec PR (lane 3, fix round B, 2026-11-09), and so do
  T101 and T102, whose outputs are in it since round B4 (lane 3, #165
  6745826156).
- **Groups A and B and the service run beside each other.** The release tag
  waits for the service: Brett's "4A" (lane 3's #165 comment 6726766577,
  which quotes his typed words "4A, 5A, 1 let them stand, 3A"; FR-058).
- **Group B** codes now, against `contracts/register-cli.md`, the fake `psql`
  and a stub `lanes-index` that answers `primary`. It lands after slices 1a
  and 1b (code#16, code#17) and after group A's subcommands, T111 to T114.
  Lane 4's spine, T412 → T413 → T414 → T415 → T417 → T418, starts when
  slices 1a and 1b land; T416 runs beside it, after T415 and T321
  (L4:421–446).
- **Group C** starts on T415 and T344, and T328 lifts Q6(c)'s refusal
  last. Lane 4's T416, the renderer's fork, lands after T321 (L4:247); the
  skeleton's order at SK:827 is superseded (lane 3, fix round B, 2026-11-09).
- **A file's pull requests** land one at a time, under Rule 6's `HOLD`. The
  code branch takes `main` by merge commit, never a rebase or a force-push.

## Phase 1: Group A, on code `main` (phase N of the test plan)

**Goal**: everything that runs through none of `lanes-edit.sh`'s three write
seams. `lanes-index` refuses `mode=primary` throughout.

**Independent Test**: the tests lane 2's map places in phase N, those of
T210 to T226 and T229 to T233, each run by the node ids its `verify:` names,
with `TMPDIR=/tmp OPENREPOTOOLS_REQUIRE_PG=1 bash tests/run.sh`. A
`-k 'sql_register or lanes_index or wip_init'` would also take phases W and
G. Files mode is unchanged: test 1's files-mode twin is phase W's, lane 2's
T240.

### Tests for group A (write first)

- **L2-N1 to L2-N8**, in lane 2's block: Table 1's L2-N rows, in "Lane 2's block", below.

### Implementation for group A: lane 1

- [ ] T103 [US1] [O] Question 11, as group A's first `lanes-index` pull request (FR-010; L1-A2): every `psql` gets its connection from `PG*` variables, never from `-d <url>` — files: `lanes-index` — verify: `TMPDIR=/tmp OPENREPOTOOLS_REQUIRE_PG=1 bash tests/run.sh -k 'test_lanes_index.py or repo_hygiene'`
  - **Source:** L1:101.
  - **Depends on:** SPEC-PR; lane 2's T202, in T103's pull request: L2-0c (`FAKE_PSQL` from `PG*`), in the same PR or before (L1:505). It is group A's first `lanes-index` PR.
  - **Changed at assembly:** lane 3, round B9, 2026-11-09: block v4.11's :108, `PGSERVICE` among the dropped variables, as T103 built them (#165 6752115241, 6754645319).
  - **Sites:** `PostgresStore._env` and `PostgresStore._run`. `_run` passes `-d self.url` today, and `_env` defaults `PGCONNECT_TIMEOUT` to 5.
  - **The mapping is SVC-2's (Y:3798–3819),** in one function that the service (U5) reuses:
    - host, port, database and user to `PGHOST`, `PGPORT`, `PGDATABASE` and `PGUSER`;
    - the query parameters `sslmode`, `sslrootcert`, `sslcert`, `sslkey`, `target_session_attrs` and `connect_timeout` to their `PG*` twins;
    - any other query parameter, or a password, is refused with 2.
    - A `connect_timeout` under 2 is refused; with none, it is 2 on register paths.
  - **Dropped from the inherited environment:** `PGPASSWORD`, `PGPASSFILE`, `PGSERVICEFILE`, `PGSYSCONFDIR` and `PGSERVICE`, as T103 built them (`PG_ENV_DROPPED`, `lanes-index:271` at `a3e1ddee`; lane 3, #165 6752115241). `PGPASSFILE` is the configured file or `_no_password_file()`, as today.
    - `PGSERVICE` is set only on the bootstrap's and `login`'s admin path (T109, T110).
  - **On register paths,** `PGCONNECT_TIMEOUT` is set, not defaulted. Each path gets D9's client timer, as `subprocess.run`'s `timeout`, in place of today's fixed 300 s.

  - **The TLS refusal at load** (lane 3's R09 leaves it to lane 1; lane 1 takes it):
    - `load_config` refuses, as a refused configuration, a `register_url=` or `reader_url=` whose host is a TCP host that is not loopback, unless its query carries `sslmode=verify-full` and an `sslrootcert=` that names an existing regular file.
    - `sslrootcert=system` is refused at such a host: C7 says never `system` for a private root (Y:2324).
    - **Allowed without TLS:** a Unix socket (no host, or a host that decodes to an absolute path) and a loopback host (`localhost`, `127.0.0.0/8` or `::1`). So the fixture's socket cluster and its 127.0.0.1 timeout stand-ins keep working.
    - **Its scope:** `reader_url=` is checked only where `register_url=` is set. A file with no `register_url=` is #160's and loads as today (US1). Today's tests configure `reader_url=postgresql://lanes_reader@db.invalid/qa` with no TLS.
    - The refusal names the key and what is missing, and prints no URL.
    - **Its exit code** is a refused configuration's, as the contract's §2.5 and §2.7 fix it:
      - 2, as a refused configuration;
      - 3 on `journal --write-id` (CO-3);
      - 1 under `register read --for guard`. That is O14, which lane 5's review closed (#165 6738431117): the guard reads a 1 as files mode under form 2, the prefetch, and as a log that could not be read under form 4.
    - FR-011 is still enforced by `ready`'s check 11 (T119) and the server's `hostnossl` reject (lane 3's R09).
  - **The TLS case,** `tls_at_load` `[J3]`, which lane 1 asks lane 2 to place in phase N. It drives each item through `lanes-index mode`, with `mode=index` in the file. That command exits 2 on a refused configuration (T105), so the expected 2s hold, and a file that loads prints `index` and exits 0:
    1. `register_url=` at a TCP host that is not loopback, with no `sslmode`: 2;
    2. the same with `sslmode=verify-full` and `sslrootcert=system`: 2;
    3. the same with `sslrootcert=` a missing path: 2;
    4. the same with a root file: it loads;
    5. a socket URL, and a `127.0.0.1` URL, with no TLS: each loads;
    6. a #160 file with no `register_url=`, whose `reader_url=` names a TCP host with no TLS: it loads as today.
  - **`[X: hosting]`:** every real URL names `<endpoint>`, which is not loopback, so the refusal reaches every workstation's file.

A `remove` deletes them for every line of its path. A `rename` deletes the old path's `object_holds`, and moves `log_lines.file` and `log_lines.lane` to the new file's lane. A `rename` also moves a lane-kind object to `lane:` and the new file's lane, as `parse_log` derives it. Step 7 then inserts what the entry sends, and an entry carries no deletes. Step 5a still runs first. Where step 6 deletes a lane's hold and the entry sends no hold on that object (case 6a), step 7 still re-marks `superseded_by` on every stored row of the object, as for an object the entry sends, and stamps no `seq` or `mode`; step 5a adds `objects:` for every lane holding an object whose hold sits on a line the entry replaces, deletes, removes or renames, so that the flip is reported (lane 3's ruling, #165 6771318480). After each, the tables equal the import's derivation of the mirror.     - 2: the schema is newer than this build's version;
    - 2: a role holds a privilege outside its scope;
    - 2: `store=sqlite`.

    - **A point-in-time restore** carries the roles, their verifiers, memberships and floors, and the database's privileges. `--roles-only` prints `unchanged` for each, and no password line. T110's `rotate` follows.
    - **The dump's route,** in lane 3's ruled order (#165 6739605144), as lane 3's `research.md` ERR-03 refines its step 1. It corrects Y ops §2.3's step 6 and §2.4, which 007's `research.md` records as lane 3's errata:
      1. the register's database exists, owned by the login Brett's admin service names: on a new CNPG Cluster, the install's `bootstrap.initdb` made it with that login as its `owner` (Y ops:141–142, :190, :371–372; Y:2283); where none exists, as on AT-LRMPG's `stoppable_cluster`, the admin creates it, as his own act, through his own maintenance connection, and so owns it. Step 2 refuses with 2 a database the admin does not own (`pg_has_role(current_user, datdba, 'USAGE')`);
      2. `bootstrap --roles-only`;
      3. T110's `login add <engineer>` for each engineer, and `login add --dump-job`;
      4. `pg_restore` into that database;
      5. `bootstrap`, which prints `unchanged`.
      - Steps 2 to 5, the `pg_restore` among them, run through one admin service, which names the register's database. `lanes-index` never picks a database.
      - **Why this order:** under option (a), `--roles-only` runs the database-level REVOKE and GRANT CONNECT, which need the database to exist, and `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` applies only to the database it is connected to.
    - **The re-import's route** is `bootstrap --roles-only`, as ops:371 runs it before a re-import, then `bootstrap`, then T115; `--roles-only` is safe to repeat, and prints `unchanged` where the roles are already in place (ops:371) (the T341 draft's item 3, `IMPL-007-T341-draft-report-20261111`).
  - **Option (a): `--roles-only` also runs the three database-level statements no database dump carries,** as it carries no role. Lane 3 ruled it (#165 6727022920). The option "--roles-only runs them (Recommended)" was answered in a dialog in lane 1's session at 21:01:25Z, which opened on an accepted prompt suggestion, "ask me the CNPG choices with multichoice" (#165 6727094958); it is not taken as a ruling here, and nothing rests on it (research.md's ERR-03). The three:
    - Y:2243's `REVOKE ALL ON DATABASE … FROM PUBLIC`;
    - Y:2244's `GRANT CONNECT`, to the writer and reader groups and the indexer's login;
    - Y:2256–2257's global `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE lanes_register_owner REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTIONS FROM PUBLIC`.
    - **Why:** lane 1's dump of both schemas held no `ON DATABASE` statement and no global default privilege (`165-managed-pg/out/cnpg-dump.sql`, 0 of each). Without them, PUBLIC would keep `initdb`'s default CONNECT on the new Cluster's database. Set before the restore, the default privilege also covers the functions the restore makes, and the dump's own `REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION` lines cover them in any case (lane 3's reason).
    - Each is idempotent, and prints `created` or `unchanged` as the roles do. The later `bootstrap` re-runs none of them, since the restored switch is at `primary`.
    - `research.md` records the erratum on Y ops:411's "only the roles". That text is lane 3's.
  - **The case,** `dump_restore_privileges` `[J3]`, which lane 1 asks lane 2 to place in phase N, with `tls_at_load` and `rotate_dump_job`:
    1. dump both schemas of a bootstrapped fixture database with rows in it;
    2. into a new database the admin created first, run `--roles-only`, then T110's `login add` and `add --dump-job`, then the restore, then `bootstrap`, all through one admin service naming that database (lane 3's order, #165 6739605144);
    3. PUBLIC holds no CONNECT on the new database, and a role outside the groups cannot connect;
    4. the writer and reader groups and the indexer hold CONNECT;
    5. the owner's global default privilege is there: a function the owner makes afterwards is not executable by a role outside the groups;
    6. `bootstrap` after the restore changes nothing: it re-runs no statement and writes no label.

    1. the plain form, the readers' prefetch: `--prefetch --cache <file>`, with `--files <set>` or `--all`;
    2. `--for guard`, the guard's prefetch, with its `--pid`, `--repo` and `--lane=`;
    3. form 3, `--since <n>`, the second read for every other primitive on primary: it returns the cached paths changed in `(n, n′]`, found by their digests at `n′` against `.head`, a cached `.paths`, the files of a cached set member and the `absent=` keys, and where any changed, it refetches the family at `n′` (Y:4900; contract draft 4.7 §3.6, T511's OP-7 and lane 1's U5 S3). It asks the service first, as forms 1 and 2 do. It does not run under `--for guard`; `.head` names no member, so `--all`'s logs count as cached through `.paths` only: a log created since `n` refetches the family, and its `.log.<key>` is written by the first read that asks for it (lane 3, #165 6778890211, T113 SHOULD-2);
    4. form 4, the guard's second read: `register read --for guard --at <n> --pid <pid> --cache <file> --lane=<key>`. `<n>` is `.head`'s `n`, `--pid` gives the client `T0` (B1), and `--cache` names the cache, as every other form does (B2). Form 4 has no `--repo`, and `--lane=` takes any key.
  - **The `=` form** (L1-D1, lane 1's decision): `register read`'s `--lane` (forms 2 and 4) and `--log-key` take their value only as `--lane=<key>` and `--log-key=<key>`, so that a key beginning with `-`, which is outside `check_lane_name`'s class, is never read as a flag. A caller must pass the `=` form, and the parser takes everything after the first `=` verbatim.

    - it resolves `<key>`'s log path case-insensitively in the images at seq `n` (Y:4160), never in the mirror (Y:4161);
    - it reads directly, never through the service (B7, ruled by lane 3, #165 6738474549);
    - it takes the generation from `.head`'s `generation=`, and ends 69 where `.head` is missing or the database's generation differs (B3);
    - it writes that one log's `.log.<key>` and `.logpath.<key>` for a key in `check_lane_name`'s class (Y:4956), and `.log.%<hex>` and `.logpath.%<hex>` for any other key (O18, below);
    - it refetches nothing else. It writes no `.head` (lane 5's B8, declined) and no `.outage`, reads no declaration, and never opens the mirror path (B4);
    - its bounds ship in T113: `--pid`, the cap at `T0` + 3.75 s and D9's guard row. T507's client half applies `T0`, the clamp and the cap, and T507's verify proves the cut (lane 5, #165 6739068493). Having no declaration, form 4 has no declaration cap and no floor (B5);
    - its cost, on that path only, is one more `lanes-index` start, plus `psql`'s start, connect and query: about 83 ms at a round trip of 0, and about 882 ms at 100 ms (Y:4365–4366; B6).
  - **It returns** the text at `n` (at `n′` in form 3) with per-file digests, and the `n` and generation it read, in one snapshot, as the register reader. Form 4 returns only the one log at the `n` it is given, and writes no digest, `n` or generation, since `.head` stays as form 2 wrote it. Its first line is `mode index` or `mode primary`. In `mode index` it reads and writes nothing.   - **The eleven cases** (W:5444–5456):
    - the answer `2` itself: "BINDING", exit 2;
    - the child live inside its bound: "BINDING IN PROGRESS", exit 2, record unchanged; the text drops "at the latest" where `bounded=no`;
    - the child live past `deadline_epoch`: "THE BIND HAS RUN PAST ITS `<bound_s>` s BOUND", with the `bounded=yes` and `bounded=no` texts;
    - another session wanting the lane: that session's own table;
    - after success: "MOVED" once, with one line per owed note, the record removed, the next prompt 0;
    - after a failure below the third attempt: the why, `name-drift`, `bind=k`, the window renamed back to `<from>`, the offer kept;
    - after the third failure: choice 2 no longer offered, and the window renamed back to `<from>` (ERR-23);
    - no restart by itself: each `2` is one attempt, at most three per offer;
    - stale (same host and container, the pid reused, or dead with no process left in the session it led): exit 2 once, `name-drift`, `rc=unknown`, `ended_utc` the guard's time, the window renamed back to `<from>` (ERR-23); the five copies are T510's;
    - `binding` from another host or container: exit 2, unchanged until `deadline_epoch` plus 60 s, then stale with no copies;
    - an unreadable record: dropped, with today's text.
  - **`guard_answer`'s choice 2** (`:11286`). The three refusals and the window's rename stay in the hook. In place of today's in-hook `lane-start` and `"$RESOLVED" set-row-state` (`:11409`):
    - it rewrites the offer file to `mode=binding` by temporary file and rename;
    - it forks `guard-bind` through `$detach` (`setsid`, else python3's `os.setsid`, else `nohup` alone), with `umask 077`, `TMUX_PANE` set, stdin on `/dev/null`, fds 3 to 9 closed and the output in a 0600 file per attempt, `<offers>/<uuid>.bind.<k>.out` (W:5342–5344, W:5357);
    - it prints "BINDING … detached (pid `<p>`, bounded at `<bound_s>` s)" and exits 2. The hook makes no register call of its own for answer 2. The `<bound_s>` it prints comes from the same helper `guard-bind` runs at its start, which computes it from the record, so the two never differ (W:5446, W:5385).
  - **`guard-bind <uuid>`**, the outer (W:5345–5356). It is a new internal verb, not a register writer, so it is not in the write guard's list (`:15537`). Its read-door class is `none` (T504). Its exit codes join the file's exit table (`tests/test_repo_hygiene.py:2735`). It:
    - refuses unless the record says `mode=binding`;
    - rewrites the record with its own `$$` and `pidstart`;
    - computes `bound_s` (below);
    - starts `timeout -k 10 "$(( bound_s - 10 ))" "$RESOLVED" guard-bind --body "$G_ID" &`, or `LANES_BIND_BODY` in its place under the seam (R48), and waits, again after a trapped signal, with a `TERM` trap that forwards to that pid;
    - after `timeout` returns 124 or 137, or after its trap fired, sends `TERM` to the group `timeout` led, then `KILL` 10 s later if any member is left, before it writes the outcome (point 39(a); routed to lane 5 with lane 4 by 6726110116, and confirmed by lane 4 in 6726296119; R47); the sweep ends by `bound_s` at the latest, inside `deadline_epoch` (W:5378);
    - where `timeout` is missing: `bounded=no`, the body started under `set -m` so it leads its own group, and the trap's `kill -TERM -- -<body pid>` then `KILL` 10 s later (W:5351–5355; probe P3's lesson that a trap signalling its own group runs again).
  - **The body**, `guard-bind --body`: `lane-start --no-launch --bind "$G_ID" <args>` through `guard_lane_start` (`:10496`); after a 0, the `set-row-state` on the lane the window left; then `guard_bound_check`'s read (`:11264`).
    - The `set-row-state` goes through `LANES_EDIT` where set, else `$RESOLVED` (R48).
    - In phase 1 on primary, the writers' placeholder (T409) answers 69 there, and the record carries the note. Phase 2's `LANES_ACT` and `LANES_EXPECT` on that call are T510's.
  - **The outcome** is written by the outer (W:5434–5438): `bound` with `rc=0`; `bind-failed` with `rc` and `why` = `lane-start`'s last refusal line; `rc=124`, `137` or the signal's code, with "stopped by its bound" or "stopped by a signal" and "what it wrote is not known".
  - **`bound_s`.** One helper computes it from the record, for the hook's "BINDING" text and for `guard-bind`'s start. Here the sum's defaults give 500 s: `c` = 2, `n_a` = 1, `w_s_ms` = 0. In bash 3.2's integers, `bound_s = (437000 + 28000·n_a·c + 19·w_s_ms + 9999) / 10000 · 10`. `LANES_BIND_BOUND_S` overrides it, and `deadline_epoch` is the start plus `bound_s` plus 5 s (W:5378). T507 makes the sum read `c`, `n_a` and `w_s_ms` from the record (open point 3).

  - **Exits:** 0, or 1 for `register-grep` with no match. 69 when the read fails, in every mode, so that `lane-worktrees` deletes nothing (Y:4926). 2 for a usage error, or a path outside the set, `lanes/log/README.md` among them. An absent `lanes/aliases.tsv` or `lanes/log/<File>.md` is 0 with nothing on stdout, in every mode; a missing `lanes/LANES.md` stays 69 (lane 3's item 9, #165 6746035594). Those ends are `register-text`'s. `register-grep` keeps `git grep`'s: a path that is absent matches nothing, 1, `lanes/LANES.md` included, as today on `origin/<branch>`, and on primary a path the door cannot give is 69, as above (lane 3, #165 6808937880, batch e Q96).

  - It holds:
    - `objects/<digest>`, content-addressed and immutable, written by temp file and `os.replace`;
    - `seq/<n>` manifests, path to digest;
    - a `head` file that moves only forward, under a short `fcntl.flock` in `SyncLock`'s form.
  - A reader uses an object only when its name equals the digest the database gave at `n` (D17). A missing object is fetched whole, and a reader copies into its own per-invocation file.
  - Collection runs under the flock. It removes objects that are in none of the last N manifests and are older than 10 minutes (PC-1). Y gives N only as "the last few manifests" (Y:4963), and neither research.md's PC-1 nor any block set it: N = 3 (lane 3's ruling, 6746004829).
  - It offers one API, which this task fixes: the contract's §1 leaves the image cache's API out of scope, and fixes only what `register snapshot --tree` puts in the tree (L1:85, :503). Lane 1's `register snapshot --tree` (T111) and `register read --prefetch` and form 4 (T113) call it. The service's write-through (T512) uses it too.
  - **The API** (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746004829; its `tasks.md`, `design.md` and `contracts/register-cli.md` citations are at `a138b1b1`, where `design.md` keeps Y's line numbers), five functions in `lanes-index`, over `state_dir()/register-images/` (`design.md:4857`; `tasks.md:892`). A digest is a file's sha256 as 64 lowercase hex, the form of the journal's `journal_files` rows (`design.md:5300`; `contracts/register-cli.md:454`).
    - `image_cache_get(digest: str) -> bytes | None`: the bytes of `objects/<digest>`, or `None` where the object is missing, unreadable, or its bytes do not hash to `digest`. The caller passes the digest the database gave at `n`, so an object is used only when its name equals it (D17; `tasks.md:899`). Checking the bytes again on read is lane 3's ruling (6746004829).
    - `image_cache_put(data: bytes) -> str`: writes `objects/<sha256(data)>` by temp file and `os.replace` where it is missing, with no lock, since two writers of one digest write the same bytes (`design.md:4963`; `tasks.md:896`), and returns that digest.
    - `image_cache_head() -> tuple[int, dict[str, str]] | None`: the cache's seq, from `head`, and its manifest `seq/<seq>`, path to digest; `None` where there is none. The caller brings those images to `n` with the journal's entries since that seq, and checks them against the digests at `n` (`design.md:2512–2516`).
    - `image_cache_record(n: int, manifest: dict[str, str]) -> None`: under the cache's flock, in `SyncLock`'s form (`design.md:4963`, which cites `code/lanes-index:1539–1606`; `tasks.md:898`), it writes `seq/<n>` by temp file and `os.replace`, moves `head` to `n` only where `n` is above it, and then collects (below).
      - A manifest may hold only the paths its writer read at `n`. A second record of the same `n` merges its paths into the first. Lane 3's ruling (6746004829).
      - The manifest's lines are `<path>` TAB `<digest>`, sorted by path bytewise, like the contract's canonical rows (`contracts/register-cli.md:452–455`). `head` holds the decimal seq. The lock file is `register-images/lock`. All three are lane 3's ruling (6746004829).
    - `image_cache_collect() -> None`, which `image_cache_record` runs under the same flock: it removes the objects named in none of the last N manifests whose mtime is over 10 minutes old, and every manifest older than the last N (`design.md:4963`; PC-1, `research.md:673`; `tasks.md:900`). N = 3 (lane 3's ruling, 6746004829). Removing the older manifests is lane 3's ruling (6746004829).
    - **Errors:** none escapes. The cache is advisory, since correctness rests on the digests and the head check (`design.md:2516`; `contracts/register-cli.md:875` in draft 4.13, `:874` at `a138b1b1`). An `OSError`, a busy lock or a corrupt file is a miss or a skipped write, and the caller fetches whole (`tasks.md:899`). Taking the flock with `LOCK_NB` and skipping the record when the lock is held is lane 3's ruling (6746004829).
    - **Readers copy:** each caller writes what `image_cache_get` returns into its own per-invocation file (`design.md:4963`; `tasks.md:899`), never a hard link into the cache.
    - **A head above the database's `n`,** as after a re-import, is not brought forward: the call fetches whole. That follows from the digest check; spelling it out is lane 3's ruling (6746004829).
  - **Who calls what:**
    - **T111, `register snapshot --tree`:** `image_cache_head`, then `image_cache_get` per path at `n`; `image_cache_put` for each file it fetched whole; `image_cache_record(n, …)` last (`tasks.md:382`).
    - **T113, `register read --prefetch` and form 4:** the prefetch with T302's `image_cache_*` functions, in its own one request (OP-15; lane 3, #165 6771318480); form 4 calls `image_cache_get` for its log at `n`; the detached whole-file fetch that form 4's direct read starts, which lane 5's T507 places, calls `image_cache_put` and `image_cache_record` (`design.md:4231–4236`; `tasks.md:422`, `:736`).
    - **T512, the service's write-through:** `image_cache_get` where its own images miss the digest at `n`; after a background whole fetch, `image_cache_put` and `image_cache_record` (`design.md:4044–4056`; `tasks.md:1424`, `:1432`).
  - **The cache signature** (lane 1's proposal, #165 6755040818, which lane 3 accepted in 6755051914, extending 6746004829). Lane 1 builds it as one commit on T301's branch, after T301's fix round:
    - `register_images(journal, edits, files, *, start=None)`: with `start`, it replays entries `c+1` to `k`; an import among them sets `start` aside, and the replay begins at the latest import. Without `start`, T301 is unchanged;
    - `register_render_queries(k, *, since=None)`: the digest query stays every path's latest `journal_files` digest at or below `k`, so an untouched path is proven by the same row;
    - `register_cache_state`, which misses on any empty or non-UTF-8 image, so an estate with an empty file in its render set always replays in full; the ruled manifest (`<path>` TAB `<digest>`) stays as it is;
    - `register_render_read(store, k, *, start=None, row="render")`: without `start` it returns the rows, `(journal, edits, files)`, typed for `register_images`; with `start`, the images at `k`, `{path: bytes}`, each proven, so its return type depends on `start`, since the retry sits beside `register_images`' proof (T301's doubt 1, accepted: lane 3, #165 6768006915; `lanes-index:1717` at `e4d28a5`). `guard` raises `ValueError` before any request (`15df461`), and no task adds it. T113 calls neither its `read` nor its `guard` row (T301's doubt 2; lane 3, 6768006915 and #165 6771318480).
    - **The proof stays T301's,** over every image at `k`. With `start`, a failed proof or an edit that does not apply is a miss, and the call is repeated once without `start`; only the full replay's failure ends 69. A cache from a reused seq costs one full read, and is never trusted.
    - **The callers' order** (T111's `--tree`): `image_cache_head`; `image_cache_get`, re-hashed, where any miss means a full replay; `register_cache_state`; `register_render_read` with `start`; `image_cache_put` and `image_cache_record`. A head above `k` is never brought forward. T113's reads are not callers: each form's read is one request from the one SQL builder that reads the switch, the readers' window and `n` with the text (tasks:455; OP-15), and `register_render_read` needs `k` before its request and reads no switch (lane 5, #165 6771208498; lane 3, #165 6771318480).
    - **The test:** lane 2's T210 takes one case with `start`, so that both arms are covered.

The parent writes `act` from `LANES_ACT` and `LANES_EXPECT` (lane 4, #165 6755236282, point 4; lane 3, 6755386771, reading 4): `act` holds `LANES_ACT`'s three parts verbatim, and one `expect=` per `LANES_EXPECT` scope in the order given, none dropped or merged. Whoever removes a record removes the directories its `call` lines name. A run that writes a record first removes each `<run>/` under `register-writes/` that is older than the record's maximum age, that no record's `call` line names, and that is not this run's own, its replay's fresh segment included (lane 3, #165 6779688808, Q15), as Y:5183 purges the marks. It removes only a 0700 directory of this uid under `<state>/register-writes/` (lane 3, #165 6769061039, T415's question 5). A run ended by a `die` before its end leaves its `<run>/` to that purge (lane 3, #165 6770820384, T415 Q5). Where no `<run>/` was made, the lookup's answer files in `<d>/` go at the run's end (REV418b NIT-1; lane 3, #165 6774499606).
  - **A 69 at the first snapshot** (open point 38, taken under R26; Y's spec:318 and :338 name the generation where the write has an id, and say that nothing was sent where it has none). What the note says depends on whether the run had an id:
    - **A run that passed `--write-id new`,** having been given no `LANES_WRITE_ID` id and having taken over no record:
      - no id was minted, since the mint is at that snapshot;
      - no record was written, since it comes only after that snapshot (Y:5192).
      - Its printed rerun is the command as it was given: no `LANES_WRITE_ID` or `LANES_WRITE_GENERATION`, or `LANES_WRITE_ID=new` again where that was given, since the record it is to replace is still there.
      - The note says that nothing was sent (spec:318).
    - **A run that had an id** prints it again with its generation: `LANES_WRITE_ID=<id> LANES_WRITE_GENERATION=<g> …`.     - **The text:** "an identical write is being started in pid `<p>` on `<host>`/`<container>` (claimed `<utc>`); its record is not yet written, so its write id is not yet known; this run sent nothing. Once pid `<p>` has ended, look for its effect in the register: `lanes-edit.sh history <lane> --since <utc>` for a log line, or `lanes-edit.sh register-row <lane>` for a row. If it is there, that write was made: do not run this command again. If it is not, run the same command again; it takes over that write's record if one was left, or writes anew."
    - **Why not a plain rerun:** if the other run finished with 0, its record is gone and its done mark is keyed by write id, so a plain rerun would write anew (Y:5182).
    - **Why not the done mark:** finding the other write's id from its done mark would rest on a pid with no start time (Y:5181 records pid, host and container), and the mark carries no digest, so it could not show the mark is this command's.

    - S3's step 5 refuses every mode except `publish` over unpublished commits (`:2649–2656`). Step 5 runs before the write's commit; a commit made on top after it meets the backstop's pinned tip, which `write-handoff` reaches once T504 gives it its mode (lane 3, #165 6770820384, T420 S1).
    - So `commit_publish`'s own push, `refs/heads/<b>:refs/heads/<b>` (`:3160–3165`), sends exactly the write's one commit.
    - The publish act, `cp_publish_act` (`:3577`), runs only when nothing is staged and `CP_PUBLISH_AHEAD=1` (`:3122–3125`), and only `commit` sets that (`:17801`). It pushes only its fenced tip, `CP_FENCE_TIP` (`:3597`).
  - **What reaches `:3165` on primary.** Verbs stop at the seam (T414), and the carry form is refused above. The one commit that still pushes at `:3165` on primary is T422's handoff move, which `commit_publish` makes with staged paths, outside the flag.
  - **The backstop:** at `:3160`, on primary and outside the flag, T420 refuses the push with 2 unless every path the pushed commit changes is under `handoffs/` (each commit in `origin/<b>..<tip>`, read against its parent with `--no-renames`; a merge or root commit refuses), and the push sends exactly that `<tip>` (lane 3, #165 6769061039, T420's question 3). T422's move passes, and anything else is refused. A D, T, symlink or gitlink under `handoffs/` passes only in the write's own commit; there a bytecode or tool-cache path passes only as a deletion, #162's cleanup (lane 3, #165 6778890211, T420's new question 1); both reads use `-z`, and on primary refuse a `.`, `..` or `.git` component (lane 3, #165 6770820384, REV420 N1, N2, N5). The refusal withholds `commit`'s publish act, and gives S6.3's archive steps alone, where the write's own commit holds a change under `handoffs/` that the act's fence refuses in any queue: one that is not an added or modified regular file (a deletion, T422's move's among them, a type change, a symlink or a gitlink), or a bytecode or tool-cache path other than as a deletion (lane 3, #165 6780753157, Q31, Q45; lane 3, #165 6808141050, batch c Q50, Q51). Every other path it refuses, in that commit or another, keeps the offer, whose words say the act refuses with 2 any other queue, and the archive steps follow it (batch c Q50).

  - **Ownership.** Lane 4 agrees that this half is lane 5's (OB-8; GB:450, GB:642), and its draft's GB-14 and GB028 leave lane 4's block.
  - **`act`** is written at the offer: the guard's base `<act-id>@<n>:<generation>`, from the `n` and generation of the prefetch that made the offer (W:5378; T410's grammar).
  - **The five copies.** When the outer writes an outcome other than 0, it copies `write_id`, `generation`, `not_after`, `write_lane` and `args_sha256` from this session's `start-record` pending record, where that record holds a `call` line (T412's `contracts/pending-record.md`).
    - The stale row takes the same copies, with `not_after` the largest among its `call` lines (v3f's case 20).
    - A record from another host or container gets none.
  - **The lookup before a rerun** (W:5460): `lanes-index journal --write-id <id> --not-after <t> --generation <g> --lane <write_lane> --verb start-record --args-sha256 <h>` (T114). A record with no `write_id`, or one whose `write_id` no pending record still holds, makes no lookup: it drops its copies and binds anew.
  - **`w₀`.** Where the record's `not_after` may not have passed, the child waits on its own clock until `ended_utc` plus `C`, then calls. `C` is `N_a`·`c` + 6 s from the record's `c` and `n_a` (R05; open point 3), not a fixed 8 s. The record's `c` is `.head`'s, the larger of the two URLs' (O15), so `w₀` waits no shorter for it.
  - **The reruns table** (W:5462–5468):
    - found: run the pending record's `cmdline` under its `write_id`, generation, lane and session, and the tail runs (T418);
    - absent, with the head row's lock granted: bind anew under a new id;
    - no try within the window, or LR011: `bind-failed`, `rc=3`, copies kept;
    - LR007 `another-write`: `rc=2`, the five copies dropped;
    - LR007 `generation`: `rc=2` with a plain rerun's refusal, copies kept.
  - **The `set-row-state` on the lane left**, after `lane-start`'s 0, with `LANES_ACT` from the record's `act` and `LANES_EXPECT="row:<from> bind:<from>"` on that call only (W:5347). Its 2 or 3 becomes a `note=`.
  - **The record's code** (v3f, "The record's code"): `start-record`'s 69, 75, 3 or 2 ends `bind-failed` with that `rc`; only a 3 keeps copies.
  - **The second run** reselects T502's names with no mode filter, so their primary half is verified once phase 2's writes exist.

divergence, rollback's and the cutover's pushes, then the constant lifted. It
changes `lanes-index` and `tests/` only (lane 4's row 10).

  - **With `render_key=`:**
    - The URL is `ssh://git@github.com/<owner>/<repo>.git`, where `<owner>/<repo>` is `_norm_repo` of the checkout's own `git remote get-url origin`, and equals `source_of`'s.
    - The push runs in an environment of its own:
      - `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` naming the key, with `IdentitiesOnly=yes` and `BatchMode=yes`;
      - `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` removed;
      - `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null` and `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`;
      - every inherited `GIT_*` variable removed;
      - `--no-verify`.
    - A non-GitHub origin is refused with 2.
  - **Without `render_key=`:** the push goes to `origin` as it stands, as under Q21(b)(A) and in the bare-origin fixtures.
  - **Both forms:**
    - The push is `<tip>:refs/heads/main`, never forced, bounded by `LANES_GIT_TIMEOUT`.
    - Nothing is written to the checkout's configuration: no `set-url` and no `pushurl`.
  - **The read-back** fetches `origin` and checks `merge-base --is-ancestor`, since a push to a URL moves no `refs/remotes/origin/main`.
  - It is security-sensitive: the key bypasses the ruleset, so the key stays in no environment a session inherits ("A server-side gate protects the rendered paths and `lanes/STORE`").

  - **Fetch.** It clears the rerun mark, and fetches `origin main` under `LANES_GIT_TIMEOUT`. A failed fetch is recorded with `record_mirror_state`, and nothing is pushed.
  - **Find `m`.**
    - It uses form C, paged with `--skip`, 200 commits a page, and takes the first non-empty `Register-Seq`. It never uses `--grep`. The whole walk is bounded by `LANES_GIT_TIMEOUT`.
    - Where it runs out of time, or finds no trailer, it records why, pushes nothing, and ends.
    - The trailer of a stray that a `stray-import` or `stray-revert` entry names does not count, through `mirror_register_seq`'s skip set (lane 3, #165 6770600106, T117's point 5).
  - **Read the head.** It reads `h` and the switch in one read. At `primary`, an `m` above `h` is a divergence at the commit `R` that carries it (R2's N6). At `files` it is not: the pass renders nothing and records no divergence, since nothing at `files` can clear one, and it ends with the outcome `at-files`, rc 0, after finding `m` (lane 3's ruling on its review's open point 2, `REVIEW-007-T322-T323-aaef9f4`).
  - **Check for a divergence.** Any commit after `R` that changes a render-set path and is not a render commit is recorded as `diverged at <sha>`, and the renderer ends. So is a render set at `m` that differs from the images at `m`.
  - **The target** is `t = min(h, m + 64, o − 1)`, where `o` is the first operator entry after `m`, by `journal.operator` (PC-3). Where `m + 1` is an operator entry, it renders nothing (F2).
  - **Build.** Under the lock, it clears `lanes-render.index` and its `.lock`. Then, for each `k` from `m + 1` to `t`:
    - it builds the images at `k` with T301's function;
    - it reads the parent's tree into the private index (`GIT_INDEX_FILE`), writes the changed blobs with `hash-object -w` and stages them with `update-index`, setting the render set exactly to the images;
    - it runs `write-tree`, then `commit-tree --no-gpg-sign -p <parent>`.
  - **The message** is the entry's message byte for byte but that its trailing blank lines are dropped and it ends with one LF (ruling 4 of lane 3's review of T322, `contracts/render-commit.md` §5), with `Register-Seq: <k>` appended to its final trailer block, or as a new last paragraph after one blank line. It never runs `git interpret-trailers`. The author and committer are the entry's, both dated by its commit UTC, with the encoding UTF-8 (D15; `contracts/render-commit.md`). Lane 1's `register commit` refuses a journal message holding a scissors line, or whose final trailer block already holds a `Register-Seq`, before it is journaled (line R's `9d68f1e`), so such a message never reaches the renderer, which checks for neither itself. Each commit built is read back by form C, and where it reads anything but `k` the build ends `build-failed` at that seq with nothing pushed, and stays there until the message is corrected; a message holding its own `k` renders, and reads back `k` (`contracts/render-commit.md` §1; lane 3's ruling on its review's SHOULD-2; `REVIEW-007-render-fixes-40a1f5e-followup`).
  - **The render set** is filtered in code, since git 2.43's `ls-tree` refuses `:(exclude)`.
  - **Push** through T320. A rejection refetches. Where origin then carries `t` or more, that counts as pushed. Otherwise the pass rebuilds, six push attempts in all.
  - It never touches a HEAD, an index or a working tree of the checkout.

## Phase 4: The register service, beside groups A and B; the release tag waits for it

**Goal**: `lanes-index serve`, SVC-1 to SVC-12, built beside groups A and B,
not after them, and the release tag waits for it: Brett's "4A" (FR-058; R35;
lane 3's #165 comment 6726766577). Act 4 waits for it too, since the guard
measured without it from Eagle is over his 1 s (Y:4826). No service pull
request lands before the spec PR. Lane 5 codes it against the fixture's
configuration while Q6(c)'s refusal stands (Y:3951–3956).

  - **Source:** L5:428.
  - **Depends on:** SPEC-PR; T511; T101; T103; T104; T105; T106; T107; T108; T109; T110; T111; T112; T113; T115; T117; T302; T504; T505; T507; lane 2's T203: L2-0d's flipped copy of lanes-index; lane 2's T237: L2-S1; lane 2's T237: A7's seven guesses and A5's status guess [J3]; lane 2's T237: A10, T512's guesses in phase N and AT-10's fresh write through T112 or commit_write; PENDING-L1: lane 1's U5 review gate; lane 2's T200, T201, T202, T203, T204, T205, T206, T207, T227: L2-0a to L2-0e (cluster, switch, FAKE_PSQL, stub lanes-index, prefixes), with T107-T110 through L2-0a's template, before any real-engine run (v5:428 (T112 only if AT-10's fresh write uses it, A10); v5:573–584, v5:586, v5:597, v5:604–605, v5:653–657, v5:661; R36, R44).
  - **Changed at assembly:** lane 3, applying: Brett's 4A (#165 6726766577); spec.md's FR-058 now says so; rule: no service PR lands before the spec PR (brief); comment 6739068493: section D item 11 also covers v5:433 (SVC-3); comment 6739068493: section D item 11 also covers v5:452 (SVC-8); section D item 11 (O16); section D item 11 (v5:455; B7); ruling R50; ruling R36; ruling R44; ruling R37 (floor 1 s; the idle check capped below 240 s; both kept in the starter's environment); lane 3, round B4, 2026-11-09: an owner file from another pid namespace or boot is unavailable and starts none (OP-8, ERR-27; #165 6746655077), and the background whole-file fetch's bounds (OP-15, ERR-29; #165 6746655077, accepting lane 1's 6746620707); lane 3, round B12a, 2026-11-10: SVC-6's start-time `psql --version` check, in lane 5's words (#165 6767221832; lane 3, 6768050756, point 3); lane 3, round B13, 2026-11-10: `LANES_REGISTER_READY_CAP_S` joins the seams' names (lane 3, #165 6774499606, T415 Q1); lane 3, round B15, 2026-11-11: the v1.3.0 tag waits for it (Brett's "v1.2.0 now, 007 later (Recommended)", 2026-11-10T13:22:23Z; #165 6768477034).
  - **SVC-1:** one service per environment, a bench container or a host shell. It connects as the engineer's reader login, `lanes_r_<engineer>`, which the environment's `lanes-index.conf` names (X5 texts 11, 12, 14).
  - **SVC-2:** the socket under `${XDG_STATE_HOME:-~/.local/state}/openRepoTools/run/`, a 0700 directory owned by this uid, not a symlink, checked on every use.
    - Peers: `SO_PEERCRED`, or `LOCAL_PEERCRED` on macOS.
    - The owner file records the pid namespace and the boot id; a client whose own differ treats the service as unavailable, and starts none (SVC-2; ERR-27).
  - **SVC-3:** `register read` asks the socket first and reads directly when the service is unavailable, but form 4, the guard's second read at `n`, always reads directly, never asks the socket and never reads `.direct` (B7, lane 3's ruling, #165 6738474549; register-service spec:59 stands). The guard's fallback line, from form 2, is `guard: direct (service unavailable)`.
  - **SVC-4:**
    - the client starts `serve` detached from Python, with `start_new_session=True`, stdin and stdout on `/dev/null`, stderr to `register.log`, working directory `/`, and a reduced environment with no URL or password (W:3849–3858);
    - one instance by `flock` on `register.lock`;
    - a stale socket unlinked under the lock;
    - a stop after 15 minutes idle, or when the install changes;
    - a failure stamp that holds back restarts for 60 s.
    - `serve --start` (R50) runs the starter and returns at once, so the bash `SessionStart` block detaches nothing itself (W:3857–3858: "No bash code detaches anything").
  - **SVC-5:** `serve` refuses unless T105's mode answer is `primary`: with 2 for files and `index` mode (W:3913).
    - Under Q6(c)'s refusal, W:4821 has `load_config` refuse it with 2 ("refuses with 2 for any subcommand but `mode` and `register read`"). So the refusing build's `serve` exits 2, as W has it, with no departure (R36; FR-003); lane 5's proposal of 78 is not taken.
    - Before T328 that is every real host, so the shipped `serve` is dormant there; its behaviour is tested on L2-0d's flipped copy (the section's opening).
  - **SVC-6:**
    - one long-lived `psql` child on pipes, connecting to the primary's port 5432 through the install's load balancer, never through a pooler (X5 text 15; lane 3 ruled that it holds, 6726110116);
    - before it listens, one `psql --version` check: a `psql` older than 15 stamps `no-psql` and exits 2, as with no `psql`, since the request's `\;` join needs 15 (contract §8.6, `socket-api.md:289`; lane 3, 6754338646, OP-10 (a));
    - a connect at `c` per address, in the background only (texts 13 and 17);
    - per-request bounds: 150 ms `lock_timeout`, 180 ms `statement_timeout`, a 200 ms timer, or the round trip plus 100 ms over 100 ms (W:3488). The round trip is `register_rtt_ms=`, which text 8″, now taken into X (6726078173), makes the p90 of act 3's `select 1` round trips in one session, taken while the workstation is at work. The timer's rule does not change, only how act 3 measures its input, so `test_the_services_request_timer_follows_register_rtt_ms` reads `register_rtt_ms=` as that p90 and keeps its fixed cases (none, and 150);
    - the background whole-file fetch: `lock_timeout` 1000 ms, `statement_timeout` 10 s, an 11 s timer; dropped on expiry, retried on the next miss (OP-15, ERR-29);
    - `SELECT 1` every 60 s, inside the load balancer's 4-minute idle reset (text 16).
  - **SVC-7:** one REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY transaction per request; images brought to `n` and checked against the digests, else from the image cache at the digest, else `unavailable`.
  - **SVC-8, the client's part:**
    - a 300 ms wait, or SVC-6's timer plus 100 ms;
    - the `.direct` flag, which form 4 never reads (B7);
    - answers over 16 MB, or incomplete within the share, are refused;
    - `w_s` reported for `bound_s`, as `.head`'s `w_s_ms=` in whole milliseconds, wherever this `lanes-index` has `serve`, on primary, whether or not the service answered the read (O16; O15).
  - **SVC-11:** the request carries file names only, and the service reads no pid, no `/proc` entry of a lane, no session record and no tmux. So it cannot be asked for a log at an older `n`, and form 4 never asks it (B7).
  - **No test yet** for SVC-2's directory, `SO_PEERCRED` and owner-file checks, SVC-5's refusal, or SVC-4's lock, stale socket and idle stop. The second run's seven new guesses ask lane 2 for them (A7).
  - **The write-through** to T302's image cache (Question 13; FR-057).
  - **`status`'s lines** for the service and its failure stamp (W:3731, W:3904) sit in lane 1's `cmd_status` (T117). They are written here, in T117's `cmd_status`, under lane 1's review, after T117 has landed; T117 does not wait for the service (R44).
  - **Two test-only variables (R37, lane 3, 6726556972; open points 24 and 25).** v3f's open point 35 asks this, and 6726110116 routed it to lane 5:
    - `LANES_REGISTER_SERVE_IDLE_CHECK_S`, the interval of SVC-6's `SELECT 1` (W:3956–3957), default 60;
    - `LANES_REGISTER_SERVE_IDLE_STOP_S`, SVC-4's idle stop (W:3877), default 900.
    - **Each is honoured only where it is set.** Each names its interval in whole seconds, and a value that is not a positive whole number is ignored, with the default kept. `LANES_REGISTER_SERVE_IDLE_CHECK_S` keeps a 1 s floor and is capped below 240 s (R37; L1:462): a value of 240 or more is ignored, with 60 kept.
    - **The precedent:**
      - in W, the register's seconds seams are named `LANES_REGISTER_*_S`: `LANES_REGISTER_DEADLINE_S` sets D7's deadline (W:2624; v3f's open point 35 names it), `LANES_REGISTER_SEAM_CAP_S` the seam's cap (W:4950), `LANES_REGISTER_READY_CAP_S` the parent's bound on a child's time to `ready` (T415) and `LANES_REGISTER_PENDING_MAX_AGE_S` the record's maximum age (W:5132); `LANES_BIND_BOUND_S` sets the bind's bound (W:5378);
      - in today's code, `LANES_POLL_SECONDS` is an interval seam of the same kind: "the interval `request-handoff`'s wait polls the published log at (default 15). The test seam, and only that" (`lanes-edit.sh:610–611`), which the suite sets to 1 (`tests/test_lane_helpers.sh:14370`).
    - **The starter's reduced environment** (SVC-4 item 2, W:3849–3858) keeps both where they are set, or the service never sees them. W's list, "`PATH`, `HOME`, `LANG`, the `XDG_*` variables and `LANES_INDEX_CONFIG`" (W:3852–3853), names them too (R37).
    - **They shorten** AT-10's `test_the_services_idle_check_replaces_a_dropped_connection_in_the_background` and A7's idle-stop guess.
    - **Recorded in** 007's `research.md`, as R37's decision: the starter's environment gains two test-only variables.
  - **Recorded in the PR body, never asserted:** the answer time against 50 ms (`test_the_guard_answer_time_is_recorded_beside_todays_direct_guard`).
  - **Not covered by a test** (v3f §6.3): one service across containers.

Each task that waited on one now says "rests on" its ruling. The contract's last open point, O21, is closed as (a) (lane 4, #165 6741093519; lane 1, 6741183916): T217 holds T401's two exit-2 cases (O21a).

**Tiers**: lane 2 proposes them, and lane 3 tags them.

- There is no `[H]`: the code leg's `AGENTS.md` at `e3939589` has no `## Governed invariants` section.
- `[O]`: every task that touches `.github/workflows/tests.yml` or `tests/fixtures/`; the fixture; the switch; every test that pins a contract (the schema, the exit table, the bounds, the hook, the bind record, `LANES_ACT`'s grammar, the operator commands); and the roles, the logins and the secret's reach.
- `[S]`: the rest.

**Names**: each name of the test plan's is used byte for byte, but one: 512 names in 27 modules, the 26 new `test_sql_register_*.py` modules and the existing `test_tests_workflow.py`. Of the 512, TP:2222's case is renamed by lane 3's ruling (R-L4Q1, #165 6746035594 item 1), from `test_the_read_mirror_variable_lowers_only_window_lane_and_swapped_to_a_mirror_read` (T275). 72 names and some parametrize ids are new since v3f, placed at other lanes' asks and named in the plan's style; `test-map-T2xx.tsv` marks each, with its source.

- No name or parametrize id holds `guard_launch_mode` or `repo_hygiene`.
- One name of the plan's holds `test_lanes_index`: `test_lanes_index_accepts_the_register_label_key` (T218). So a regression run's `-k` names the module file, `test_lanes_index.py`, which pytest's `-k` accepts and which selects only that module (lane 1's ask; NOTES).
- Where another lane's `-k` word was a guess, the new name keeps the word: lane 1's six cases, `tls_at_load` to `journal_last_pickaxe`, and lane 5's guesses, kept whole. R04's parity test carries lane 1's `classify_parity`.
- pytest's `-k` also matches the module's name and the class's, as substrings, so each new name's `[J3]` word is one that selects only its own case. `j3-resolutions.md` collects each on the stubs (addendum 2).

**Verify**: every command is offline, and runs from the code worktree. Two forms, as R28 rules:

- **A test-writing task** collects its tests by node id with the switch at 0: `OPENREPOTOOLS_REQUIRE_PG=0 PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider --collect-only <node ids>`. pytest exits 4 when any node id or parametrize id is missing, and 0 when all are there, whatever later tasks add. It runs no test, since the tests fail until another lane's code lands; it takes no lock and writes nothing, so it needs neither `tests/run.sh` nor `TMPDIR=/tmp`. Naming each id is exact, where an `or` list in a `-k` passes with one name present.
- **A case another task adds,** which the plan gives no id, is named by the id this block gives it. Where a rule covers many tests, one case is checked by a word: `… --collect-only <node> -k '<word>'`, which exits 5 when it selects nothing.
- **A task that runs tests** takes lane 4's anchored two-run form (R28): `TMPDIR=/tmp OPENREPOTOOLS_REQUIRE_PG=1 bash tests/run.sh tests/<module>.py -k '<module word> and (<names>)'`, one run per module, then a regression run, `TMPDIR=/tmp OPENREPOTOOLS_REQUIRE_PG=1 bash tests/run.sh -k '<existing modules>'`. `tests/run.sh` always passes `tests` to pytest first, so the file argument fails the run when the module is missing, and the `-k` keeps it inside the module.
- A collect never names a function bare beside one of its ids, nor a module beside a node in it: pytest 9.1.1 then exits 0 with the id missing (NOTES). A function whose ids all belong to one task may be named bare; a function split between tasks is named by every id that is its own (R2r-N4; check 19).
- **A smoke** is a module's own entry, run directly: `python3 -I -B tests/pgcluster.py --smoke <name>…` (T205, T229).
- A `verify:` with the switch at 1 needs PostgreSQL 16's binaries: CI's runners, or a bench after Q1's 1A image rebuild.
- Never wrap `tests/run.sh` in `flock` or `timeout`: it locks itself. Run it from a shell with no `LANES_*` or `TMUX` inherited, and pause between runs.
- No test of this block carries an `xfail` or a `skip` for a task's behaviour; the only skip is a real-engine test's without the switch, which the switch at 1 turns into a failure (lane 4's Q-L2-5).

**Imports**: every test module imports cleanly before the code it checks exists. A check of another lane's code runs inside a test, never at import, since one module that fails to import makes every `tests/run.sh` run exit 2.

**Configurations**: every configuration a test writes with `register_url=` names a Unix socket or a loopback host (`127.0.0.0/8`, `localhost` or `::1`), or a TCP host with `sslmode=verify-full` and an `sslrootcert=` naming an existing root file, never `system`. Otherwise lane 1's TLS refusal at load (T104; CLI4.5 §2.5; R09) answers first: 2, or 3 on `journal --write-id`, or 1 under the prefetch. The only exceptions are the cases that test that refusal, `tls_at_load` (T230). T212's refusing state and T213's case 4 say how they meet the rule. It is lane 1's L1v4.5:525 for the fake `psql` (#165 6741670709 item 9): T203 makes the fake `psql` and the shim for such configurations only, and T202, T212, T213, T231, T245, T253 and T256, whose tests hand them one, each state it.

**Split functions**: a function whose cases the test plan spreads over phases is written in the first, and each later phase adds its cases, each named in that task's `verify:` by its full id:

- `test_mode_primary_is_refused_until_the_git_half_and_goes_to_sql_once_flipped`: `[refusing]` and `[mode-under-index-off]` in T212 (R-F9-5), `[flipped]` and `[replay-keeps-pid]` in T256 (R-T409-9);
- `test_status_and_rollback_run_with_no_lane_session_and_no_guard`: `[status]` in T219, `[rollback]` in T279;
- `test_a_connect_timeout_above_the_direct_reads_share_is_cut_at_the_parse_start_line` and `test_a_database_that_does_not_answer_is_2_inside_4_5_s`: the `service-down` cases in T213, the `service-up` cases in T237;
- `test_each_paths_connections_and_queries_are_counted`: `[m5]` in T237 and `[m1]`, `[m2]`, `[m6]`, `[m7]`, `[m9]` and `[m10]` in T255, whichever is written first making the function;
- `test_each_database_path_exits_inside_its_bound` and `test_a_slow_statement_meets_57014_before_the_client_timer`: D9's rows, in T220, T237, T257 and T278, by the row words T220 gives;
- `test_ready_check_11_needs_tls_and_the_primary`: its five ids in T224, and `[leaf-only]`, `[system-root]` and `[plaintext]` in T230;
- AT-F6's tests whose cases bind through `start-record` in both modes: the mode as each id's last part, `[files]` or `[<id>-files]` in T214, and `[primary]` or `[<id>-primary]` in T254 (A1). T214 lists the AT-F6 tests that take none.

#### Phase 0: Setup (shared infrastructure)

    - `[after-137]`, new since v3f (R47): after `[ignores-term]`'s 137, with a grandchild of the body that ignores `TERM`, the outer sends `TERM`, then `KILL`, to the group `timeout` led, both before `deadline_epoch`, and no process of the group is left;
    - `[after-trap]`, new since v3f (R47): a `TERM` sent to the outer mid-bind fires its trap; it sweeps the group `timeout` led the same way, inside `deadline_epoch`, before it ends the record, and no process of the group is left;
    - a body that ends by itself gets no sweep: the fake `kill`'s log holds no call after the body's own exit.

  - **The guard's session record (R-F10-27, R-F10-28):** each guard case calls T203c's `guard_session(home, directory, cwd, *, window=…, session_id=…)` (line H, `1e073fcf`) before it runs the guard, with its checkout under `$HOME/projects` and the compact hook JSON, and builds its environment by `guard_strip_env`, which strips `PROJECTS_ROOT` and `AGENT_PROTOCOL_ROOT` (T203). M11's bounded lookup is T121's form above with `max_pages=1`, one page of 200 at `--skip 0`, which answers `no-seq` past it (lane 1's follow-up 4, `l1-007-R-mirrorpage` `5dc989a`, in line R merge18; lane 1, #165 6798083202); T121 never uses `--grep` (`lanes-index:2714`), which stays only as a comparison. The checkout is whatever `lanes-edit.sh workspace-root` answers, `$HOME/.agents/workspace.yaml` by default.

  - **Tests:** 32: 16 from AT-C5, and 16 new since v3f: lane 1's three (`guard_second_read_at`, `log_key_hex_slot`, `journal_last_pickaxe`), lane 5's nine (A5), lane 4's two read verbs (GB §4.3), and T502's `lanes --decide` test (R38, as lane 3 amended it); and, since F13c, `lane-start`'s `--no-launch` act (R-F13c-23).
  - Each reader by the table with the store unreachable; each mirror read's line; the none-class arms; `test_every_dispatcher_arm_is_classified_for_the_read_door`, the hygiene test, which holds every arm in exactly one of six classes, `writer` among them for the write guard's verbs, `start-record` included (R46); one failed call anywhere in a caller's read sequence; 75 and 3 at `lane-start`'s seven arms, and 69 at two of them; the other callers' arms; the printed rerun relayed on a 3; `register-row-local`; `lane-worktrees`' reads; `--index` on primary; and `managed-projection`, RE on `tmpl_imported` and FP when unreachable.
  - The arms' parametrize ids are their lines at `2a057a6d`, as the test plan names them, so they move with slices 1a and 1b and are re-cited at each landing.
  - The one-call failure runs every `k`, or a sample of them: open point 14.
  - `lane` and `lane-end --retire-dormant` pass `lanes --decide` (R38; lane 4's T408 and T403). The listing test drives the callers, so it holds either way.
  - **Lane 1's three, new since v3f,** which L1v4.5's T113 and T114 ask lane 2 to place:
    - `test_guard_second_read_at_its_head_writes_one_log_and_fails_open` (`guard_second_read_at`, L1s36-1, L1v4-1; L1v4.5:301–310), form 4 after form 2 has cached the family at `n`: `[moved-head]`, with the head moved past `n` by an entry that changes the key's log, it writes the log's text at `n` and exits 0; `[case-insensitive]`, a key in another case resolves to the one `lanes/log/<File>.md`, which `.logpath.<key>` names; `[outside-the-class]`, a key outside `check_lane_name`'s class writes only `.log.%<hex>` and `.logpath.%<hex>` (O18); `[no-mirror]`, with the mirror's copy of the log holding another text and its checkout unreadable, it writes the images' text at `n`; `[generation-differs]`, `[no-head]`, `[above-the-head]` and `[below-the-first-seq]`, each 69; `[case-duplicates]`, two render-set logs that differ from the key only by case, 69 (C11); `[dash-key]`, a key beginning with `-`, passed as `--lane=<key>`, read as the key (L1-D1); `[refusal-78]`; `[refused-configuration-1]`, R09's included; and `[empty-lane-64]`, an empty `--lane=` (CLI4.5:755). In each, it writes its one log's pair or nothing, `.head` is byte-identical and no `.outage` is written, and every end but 0 writes no `.log.<key>` (B4). Its 2 for too late, which needs `T0`, is T213's `test_a_second_read_past_the_start_line_is_noted_not_refused` (S36-T507).
    - `test_log_key_hex_slot_holds_a_key_outside_the_class_and_is_never_cached` (`log_key_hex_slot`, L1s36-2; O18): `[form-3]`, form 3 with `--log-key=<key>`, for two keys outside the class, one holding `,` and one `/`, writes each its own `.log.%<hex>` and `.logpath.%<hex>`, rewritten on each read and never counted as cached; `[dash-key]`, a key beginning with `-`, passed as `--log-key=<key>`, is read as the key (L1-D1); `[forms-1-and-2]`, form 2 with an out-of-class `--lane=`, and form 1 with `--all` over a render set holding such a log, fetch no log for the key and refuse nothing (L1v4.5:312–317).
    - `test_journal_last_pickaxe_finds_the_newest_entry_that_changes_the_keys_count` (`journal_last_pickaxe`, L1s36-3; S36-C7, C8): `journal --last --path <log> --grep <key>` gives `seq=` of the newest entry whose edits change how many times `<key>` occurs in the log, as a fixed string; `[dot-in-name]`, a lane name holding `.` matches only itself; `[count-kept]`, an edit that keeps the count does not match; `[no-match]`, no `seq=` line; `[at-bound]`, `--at <n> --generation <g>`, with `.head`'s generation, bounds the search to entries at or below `n` (L1v4.5:327, :329; L1v4.5-327); `[at-needs-generation]`, `--at` without `--generation` ends 64; `[past-head-or-other-generation]`, an `n` above the head, or a generation that differs, ends 69 (L1v4.5:329–330; L1v4.5-330). Five more, new since v3f, hold lane 3's accepted readings of `--last`, for T114 (lane 1, #165 6755628366 reading 5; lane 3, 6755922262 reading 5; R-F11-1): `[generation-needs-at]`, `--generation` without `--at`, 64; `[empty-grep]`, an empty `--grep`, 64; `[path-empty-or-control]`, an empty `--path` and one holding a control character, each 64; `[at-minus-1]`, `--at -1` with `.head`'s generation, accepted, never 64, which is all the case asserts until a draft states its meaning (lane 1, #165 6767591676: draft 4.13's open point 3); and `[replay-mismatch-69]`, a journal whose replace line does not match its `old_sha256`, 69. T114's word, `sql_register_readers and journal_last_pickaxe`, selects all eleven.
  - **Lane 5's nine (A5),** its guesses kept by name: one prefetch and every read site reading its cache at one `n` (T505); a read site with no prefetched file making one more `register read`, form 3 at `n′` with the family refetched where a cached path changed, never a `git` read, with `[case-duplicates]`, 69 and no `.log.<key>` (C11), and `[failed-read]`, the class's failed read, never files mode (C3); `reg_flush` dropping the whole family and emptying `LANES_REGISTER_CACHE` (C6); `swapped`'s rank through `journal --last` (T506); `log_sync` on primary with no fetch and no flush; the local file unions skipped; the `lanes` footer naming the register when the `lanes` script asks for it with its flag, and never claiming a local read (R43; T509); `lanes --fetch` printing no no-fetch note; and `LANES_READ_MIRROR=1` lowering each read that names the lane to a mirror read on primary, and no other arm (T504; R-L4Q1: `test_the_read_mirror_variable_lowers_each_read_that_names_the_lane_on_primary`, renamed from lane 5's guess, its word `read_mirror_variable` kept; the reads are T275's list).
  - **Lane 4's two read verbs (R29; GB §4.3; T401):** `register-text` answers the bytes the scripts read today in files and `index` mode, and the image at the door's `n` on primary; `register-grep` prints what `git grep` prints, and 1 with no match; each exits 69 on a failed read, in every mode. **T401's two exit-2 cases (O21a):** lane 4 took (a) for O21 (#165 6741093519; lane 1, 6741183916), so each verb answers `lanes/LANES.md`, `lanes/aliases.tsv` and each `lanes/log/<lane>.md`, the render set's lane logs, and 2 for any other path in every mode, `lanes/log/README.md` included, and `register-grep … -- lanes/log` greps only the lane logs (CLI4.5:811; L4w11, T401, #165 6742584818). In each verb's test, `[other-path]` asserts that 2 for `lanes/repos.tsv`, and `[log-readme]` for `lanes/log/README.md`, each in files mode, in `mode=index` and on primary. On primary these, and `register-grep`'s `[log-dir-skips-readme]`, `[case-pair]` and its `[no-match]` 1, run with the store reachable, on the primary fixture or the contract stub (R2f4-S14): with the store unreachable, a failed prefetch exits 69 in the main shell before any decide arm, with nothing on stdout, so the 2 is never reached (lane 5, #165 6746507353, answer 12; L5-A12).
    - `register-grep`'s test also takes `[log-dir-skips-readme]`: with `-- lanes/log`, a line of `lanes/log/README.md` that the ERE matches is not printed, in every mode: in files mode and `mode=index`, by the pathspec `':(glob)lanes/log/*.md' ':(exclude)lanes/log/README.md'`, and on primary, whose render set holds no README (L4w11:85, T401; R2f3-N1);
    - and `[case-pair]` (lane 4's Q-W11-4, its decision (A)): on primary, two render-set logs that differ only by case, made as this task's `[case-duplicates]` cases make them (C11), make `register-grep … -- lanes/log` end 69 with nothing on stdout. Since a failed prefetch gives the same 69 with nothing on stdout, the case also asserts that the store answered: the same run's `register-text lanes/LANES.md` answers 0 with its image (R2f4-S14). That `lane-worktrees` then deletes nothing is `test_lane_worktrees_register_reads_go_through_a_verb_and_refuse_69`'s 69 arm, in this module.
    - **Their `[J3]` words,** in this module: `register_text and (other-path or log-readme)` and `register_grep and (other-path or log-readme)`, each selecting its verb's two exit-2 cases; `register_grep and log-dir-skips-readme` and `register_grep and case-pair`, one case each.   - **X, hosting:** the TLS cluster stands for the install's endpoint, whose certificate comes from the private CA that issues the endpoint's certificate, and each workstation trusts one root file (operations §2.2, item 4; design §1.4, "TLS and the endpoint"). So the fixture's test CA is a chain, not one self-signed certificate: a root, an intermediate and a leaf, made by `openssl`. The server's certificate file holds the leaf and the intermediate, as the install's `tls.crt` does, and `sslrootcert=` names the root alone. The cluster sets `ssl_min_protocol_version = TLSv1.3`, CNPG's default, and a `hostnossl` line that rejects. A start option serves the leaf alone, for T230's `[leaf-only]` case. `start_again(standby=True)` restarts it in recovery: it writes `standby.signal` in the data directory, its readiness poll waits for `ready`, as every start's does, then fails unless `pg_is_in_recovery()` is true (a hot standby writes `ready`, never `standby`: lane 3's batch 11 (1)), and the next `start_again()` without it removes the file (TP:5382–5385; T224's `[in-recovery]`) (lane 3's batch 7, #165 6780364102, `RULINGS-batch7-analysis-20261110.md` (`d316408b…`), text change 13; R-F13c-17). **Owed, T229d, with line FX9's finding (R-F13c-25):** on PostgreSQL 16 a hot standby writes `ready` to `postmaster.pid`, never `standby`, so a poll for `standby` cannot pass; line FX9 proposes "polls for `ready`, then `pg_is_in_recovery()` is true", which lane 3's batch 11 (1) rules. The `verify:` runs both (R1's M2).
  - **The delayed-loopback relay (R-P23; lane 3's ruling on open point 23, option B, #165 6741861590):** `tests/pgcluster.py` gains `delayed_loopback(target_port, delay_s)`, a relay in the standard library, needing no root, that listens on `127.0.0.1` at a free port, forwards to the TLS cluster's port, and delays each byte by `delay_s` in each direction. It passes TLS through untouched, so the client's `verify-full` meets the TLS cluster's own certificate. AT-D9c (d) (T257) and AT-F6's case 21 (T254) connect through it, at the delay and TLS each names. Its limit: it models no slow start and no loss; lane 5's netem run and act 3's rows cover those, and option A stays a bench-only check, never a CI gate.

  - **Tests:** three ids of an existing test, `[leaf-only]`, `[system-root]` and `[plaintext]`, and one name new since v3f, lane 1's `tls_at_load` (L1-1).
  - `[leaf-only]`: the TLS cluster serves the leaf without the intermediate, with `sslrootcert=` naming the root: the connect fails verification, check 11 fails and `ready` exits 2. The install's `tls.crt` must hold the leaf and every intermediate (operations §2.2, item 4); lane 1 ran both chain shapes, and a leaf sent alone failed (design §1.4, "TLS and the endpoint").
  - `[system-root]`: the URL carries `sslrootcert=system` against the private root, which no system store holds: the connect fails, check 11 fails and `ready` exits 2 (design §1.4: never `system` for a private root).
  - `[plaintext]`: a URL with `sslmode=disable` meets the TLS cluster's `hostnossl` reject, so check 11 fails naming the connection. FR-011 is enforced at check 11 and by the server's reject (R09).
  - The three run on the TLS cluster at `127.0.0.1`, a loopback host, so T104's refusal at load lets their URLs load and check 11 meets them.

    - `[no-sslmode]`, `[system-root]` and `[missing-root]`: a `register_url=` at a TCP host that is not loopback, with no `sslmode`, with `sslrootcert=system`, or with a root path that does not exist: 2, naming the key and what is missing, and printing no URL;
    - `[root-file]`: the same with `verify-full` and a root file: it loads, and prints `index`;
    - `[socket]` and `[loopback]`: a socket URL, and a `127.0.0.1` URL, with no TLS: each loads;
    - `[no-register-url]`: a #160 file with no `register_url=`, whose `reader_url=` names a TCP host with no TLS: it loads as today;

So the 57014 it timed was always the guard's direct read, and once L5-A3 and L5-S1 land it fails by its timing assertion. Its rebuild is owed by T237's next build, and the case fails until then.
  - **As built in wave 8 (R-F13b-G8, R-F13b-W3a8; line G8's T237z `d2433fbc`, T237za `e05f2fd1` and T237zb `43cb563c`, tags `l2-007-T237z` to `l2-007-T237zb`, `BUILD-W8/REPORT-LINE-G8-T237z-T237za-T237zb.md`; line W3a8's T237y `48a6d661`, tag `l2-007-T237y`, `BUILD-W8/REPORT-LINE-W3a8-T257c-T237y-T257d.md`):** **the write case** (T237z) follows the plan's "A fresh write": a request, a write, a request, the second answer equal to the direct read made after the write. A helper, `_Register`, puts the estate's `lanes/LANES.md` into the case's database as `lanes_w_fx` through lane 1's own functions, in `RealRegister`'s order: a `commit_write` import entry, a cutover entry, then `record_marker`; the write is `_Register.replace`, one SERIALIZABLE request at `register_head.seq` and `register_switch.generation` holding one `replace` edit, its `files` row carrying the sha256 of the file's whole new text (forms from `refs/l1/T112-fu2`). The case starts in the plan's `ok` state and the write moves it to `refuse`, so a cached answer cannot pass; one request first warms the service (d8:502). **The plan's ids** (T237za): `[ok|refuse|offer]` on the byte-identical function, each state written through `_Register` (`[offer]` on the module's name-drift estate, line G8's reading), and `[empty-cache|cache-holds-n]` on the cache-miss function; `_both` warms the service first, since a cold service answers `unavailable`. T237zb fits the journal's author to lane 1's `journal_author_check`, `Name <address>`, which a real-engine run on lane 5's PostgreSQL 16 found failing with 23514. **`[service]`** (T237y) starts `serve` once and warms it until a guard run is not `service unavailable`, then times the held child, recognised by `VERBOSITY=sqlstate` in its argv, through a streaming wrapper, `STREAM_PSQL`, expecting 0.18 s ≤ t < 0.36 s and the guard to exit 2; the row keeps 180 ms. Under `VERBOSITY=sqlstate` the server's timeout and the service's SIGINT both print `ERROR:  57014`, so the case cannot tell them apart (NOTES question 37). The write case fails until L1-A6 (T107), T227, T228 and L5-S1; `[ok]`, `[refuse]` and `[offer]` until L5-S1, L5-A3, T227, T228 and L1-A6; `[empty-cache]` and `[cache-holds-n]` also until T302; `[service]` until L5-S1 and L5-A3. The `verify:` now names the five ids one by one (R1's S1). It exits 4 on line G alone, since it names the measure module's `[m5]` and the bounds rows on lines M and W3a; on a scratch merge of M and W3a it collects 42, exit 0. On the built module T512's word (B12b tasks:1587) that selected 38 now selects 41, as on the stubs, by T237za's three new ids; T512's other word stays 12, T513's 1, and no other word moves.

    - **`status`'s `service` lines** are T512's, written in T117's `cmd_status` after T117 lands, under lane 1's review (R44): they report the failure stamp and its cause, and a socket directory that fails its check, which has no stamp (§8.6, from Y:3939; OP-9). The contract does not fix their words, so `test_status_prints_the_services_lines_and_its_failure_stamp` takes them by name from T512's text, which lane 5 posts with T512's build; until then it asserts a line beginning `service`.
    - **The peer credential** is `SO_PEERCRED`, read at both ends: the service closes a client of another uid, and the client treats a service of another uid as unavailable (`socket-api.md:119–120`; Y:3789–3794). On macOS it is `LOCAL_PEERCRED`, not shown (OP-12). The function's name comes with T512's build; until then the case finds a `lanes-index` function whose name holds `peercred`, lane 2's reading.
    - **A request's `statement_timeout` is 180 ms, with `lock_timeout` 150 ms,** set in `PGOPTIONS`, so the server cancels its own query just inside the 200 ms timer (`socket-api.md:214`; Y:4017–4020), one query at a time on the held connection (`:908`). Only the image fetch differs: its own transaction, with `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '1000ms'` and `statement_timeout = '10s'`, the renderer's row, under an 11 s timer (`:244`). So T220's second function's `[service]` row reads 180 ms.

  - **The tries (R26, lane 4's answer to 40):** `[timer]` and its siblings assert each try starting at least 1 s after the last one's start, never before the last one has ended, and any try starting up to and including the window's 10 s mark, on a monotonic clock. The 1 s takes a tolerance, since a try's start is stamped when its process starts, and those stamps can read below 1.0 s under load (lane 3, #165 6768006915, on lane 1's 5 of 77 end-to-end cases at a load average of about 40, each low gap next to a high one, the two summing to about 2 s, 6767591676; R-F11-24): each try starts at least 0.75 s after the last one's start, and the k-th at least k − 1.25 s after the first's, so a late stamp cannot pass a short gap twice. The exact spacing is lane 1's virtual-clock schedule check's to prove (T114). The 0.75 s and 1.25 s are lane 2's (NOTES question 34).
