  - **Tests:** 11, from AT-11 (11).
  - The mirror byte-identical after each act; no handoff commit rewriting a render commit; `lane-start` for a new lane refusing 69 before it writes, and a resume launching with a note; `start-record`'s codes; `request-handoff` and `claim` at 69; `lane-handoff`'s labelled mirror read; `LANES_READ_MIRROR=1`; the late `PAUSED` path; and `lane-end` at 69.
  - **`LANES_READ_MIRROR=1` (R-L4Q1; lane 3, #165 6746035594 item 1, "(a), widened"; YS:692; TP:2198–2204):** TP:2222's case, `test_the_read_mirror_variable_lowers_only_window_lane_and_swapped_to_a_mirror_read`, is renamed `test_the_read_mirror_variable_makes_each_read_that_names_the_lane_read_the_mirror`, its word `read_mirror_variable` kept. With the variable set and the database unreachable, every read that names the lane before the handoff file is published reads the mirror, and labels the read, where it exited 69: in `lane-handoff`, `[window-lane]`, `[swapped]`, `[register-row-window]`, `[register-row-lane]`, `[canon-lane]`, `[last-session]`, `[lane-state]`, `[set-lane-state]`, `[handoff-path]` and `[write-handoff]`, and the skill's own calls of these verbs, one id each: `[skill-handoff-path-create]`, its `handoff-path "$lane" --create` (`SKILL.md:369` at line R merge20 `4353eb08`), and `[skill-write-handoff-prepend]`, its `write-handoff "$lane" --prepend` (`SKILL.md:541`). These replace the skill's `publish-handoff`, which slice 3 removed; T406 sets the variable on these two calls, as T405 does in `lane-handoff` (R-B5-1; lane 3, #165 6747743440 ask 1, 6747422030 point 1, 6747466716). T406 also sets it on the skill's three other reads, and T275 gives each one id, all seam-free (lane 3, #165 6748705908, under 6747422030 point 2; R-F9-2; lines at line R merge20 `4353eb08`, lane 2, #165 6814402899): `[skill-register-row-sread]`, its `register-row "$lane"` through `sread` (`SKILL.md:147`); `[skill-canon-lane-sread]`, its `canon-lane "$lane"` through `sread` (`:240`); and `[skill-register-row-direct]`, its `register-row "$lane"` read directly (`:293`). Each asserts the oracle above: with the variable set and the database unreachable, the read reads the mirror and labels it. `[other-arms]` holds `who` and `lane-last` at 69 under the variable (the ruling's item 3: the verb table decides, and this case guards it). No register write rests on those answers: `PAUSED`, the LANES.md line and the row still exit 69 with their notes, and the lane stays `SWAPPING`. Lane 3's answers to NOTES question 22 (#165 6747281902) set the rest, and the test asserts only what the rulings state. Each id is verb-level: it calls its verb directly (R-Q22-2). `[register-row-window]` is `lane-handoff:1656`'s read of the window's name, before the seam, and `[register-row-lane]` is `:2521`'s read of the lane, after it (lane 3's numbers, at `ddc972d`; `:1649` and `:2514` at `a2073eae`; R-Q22-3). Each mirror read's label is YS:626's form: `read: mirror at `, 12 hex digits, and `(sql unreachable: ` (R-Q22-4). `lane-handoff:1507` and `:1529` send stderr to `/dev/null`, so those two labels reach no whole run until lane 4's T405 forwards them, and their ids assert them at the verb: by lane 2's reading, `[lane-state]` and `[set-lane-state]`, the calls at `:1500` and `:1522` at `a2073eae` (NOTES question 22). `[set-lane-state]` writes no register line: with the database down and the variable set, it exits 0 and records `SWAPPING`, and the case asserts both (R-Q22-1). `[write-handoff]`, `[skill-write-handoff-prepend]` and `[set-lane-state]` run the seam inside their verbs (R-Q22-2, whose `publish-handoff` is now the skill's `write-handoff` call; R-B5-1). Brett has ruled the seam in an outage, his list's item 9: "(i) Mirror read (Recommended)" (his answer in lane 3's session at 21:09:40Z, quoted in lane 3's #165 6752010154; R-F9-8). So they, and the whole run, `test_lane_handoff_writes_the_handoff_from_a_labelled_mirror_read_and_leaves_the_lane_swapping`, need no ruling. Their oracle is the read-mirror one the seam-free ids use: with the variable set and the database unreachable, the read goes to the mirror, with `read: mirror at `, 12 hex digits, then `(sql unreachable: `; `[set-lane-state]` still exits 0 and records `SWAPPING` (R-Q22-1). The ruling does not state the seam verbs' own output beyond that, so the case asserts nothing more of it (NOTES question 30). `[skill-handoff-path-create]` is seam-free, in lane 3's words: "`handoff-path` writes no register line and runs no seam, so `[skill-handoff-path-create]` joins the seam-free ids" (#165 6748705908; R-F9-1; NOTES question 22). The seven seam-free reads' eight ids in `lane-handoff`, `[window-lane]`, `[swapped]`, `[register-row-window]`, `[register-row-lane]`, `[canon-lane]`, `[last-session]`, `[lane-state]` and `[handoff-path]`, and the skill's four seam-free ids, `[skill-handoff-path-create]`, `[skill-register-row-sread]`, `[skill-canon-lane-sread]` and `[skill-register-row-direct]`, go now.
  - **The bind's "no row" case (Y, the sql spec's :318 and its :338 scenario; R26, lane 4's answer to 38):** a resume's note carries the writer's printed rerun, naming the write id and its generation where the write has an id, a pending record or `LANES_WRITE_ID`, or saying that nothing was sent where it has none.
    - `[69-at-the-snapshot]`: a new write's first snapshot fails, so the note says that nothing was sent and names no id and no generation, the command as given is the rerun, and the run's claim file is removed;
    - `[69-at-the-snapshot-with-an-id]`, new since v3f: the same failure under `LANES_WRITE_ID` and `LANES_WRITE_GENERATION` from an earlier printed rerun; the note names that id again with its generation;
    - `[75]`, `[3]` and `[69-at-the-commit]` name the id and its generation, as before;
    - in each, the stamp is written and the launch goes on.

#### The design's summary of this plan, in Y's words

The design's "## The test plan" summary is lane 2's v3f summary, folded byte for byte, and frozen at X and Y, so its "managed" words stay there. These are the replacements for the next re-cut of the summary (lane 3, #165 6726725691), each from the line at Y (`a6497d00`) it replaces; Y's lines are X's plus one, after SVC-5:

- **Y:6153–6154 (X:6152–6153):** "the managed PostgreSQL's bootstrap, two logins for each engineer, dump and restore (AT-LRMPG)" becomes "the bootstrap on CNPG without a superuser, two logins for each engineer made by `lanes-index login`, the dump, and a restore from the dump or from a physical copy (AT-LRMPG)".
- **Y:6156–6157 (X:6155–6156):** "and the shared database over a managed cloud link (§5.6, M12 and M13, with lane 5's netem run)" becomes "and the shared database over a remote link (§5.6, M12 and M13, with lane 5's netem run)".
- **Y:6161–6162 (X:6160–6161):** "Where the managed cloud PostgreSQL's link changes a timer or a healthy call's time, the figures are lane 4's (comment 6722143023)." becomes "Where the remote server's link changes a timer or a healthy call's time, the figures are lane 4's (comment 6722143023)."
- **Y:6170–6175 (X:6169–6174),** the open points: "The ones that stay open ask lane 3 which text holds: the timing rule's twofold margin (34), a takeover whose snapshot fails (36), the lookup timer's 8 s or 9 s (37), a 69 with no generation (38), `timeout` and a grandchild, the lookup's tries, the admin service's database on a restore, a claim with no record and a pooler (39 to 43)." becomes "X settles the lookup's timer, D9's 9 s (37), and no connection through a pooler (43). Lane 3's rulings take lane 4's answers on a takeover whose snapshot fails (36), a 69 with no generation (38, with Y's spec change), the lookup's tries (40) and a claim with no record (42), and lane 5's on `timeout` and a grandchild (39); lane 3's ruling answers the admin service's database on a restore: the database is created first, and one admin service names it (41). The timing rule's twofold margin (34) stays open."
- **Y:6164–6169 (X:6163–6168),** the coverage: after "have a row" add "; X and Y change the text of eleven lines of the two specs and rename none, so each row holds".

Lane 2's block carries these sentences here, not in the design, since the design is frozen.

#### Measurement

Section 5 of the test plan: tests 9's and 10's figures, and the timed cases of AT-1, AT-5, AT-7, AT-F6 and AT-F11 (M6, M9 and M11), go in the pull-request bodies and in `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`, never into a CI threshold. Each such test asserts correctness only. Lane 2's proposal for their CI cost, PC-15, is in NOTES: every trigger, until the first green runs measure the long rows. Act 3's recipe follows X's design, not the test plan's section 5.6: `c` comes from the p90 of a workstation's cold connects and `register_rtt_ms=` from the p90 of act 3's `select 1` round trips (design D9, SVC-6), with the medians recorded beside them; no CI test runs them. Against CNPG, CI's figures stay the local share and a floor.

## Dependencies & Execution Order

Each task's own **Depends on** bullet is the authority. This section
summarises them by phase and by lane.

### Phase dependencies

- **The spec PR** gates every landing below (6726556972). Coding starts now.
  Every task's Depends on names `SPEC-PR`, but T101, T102 and T344 ride the
  spec PR.
- **Phase 0** has no task dependency beyond the spec PR. Lane 2's cluster
  and switch, T200, T201, T205 and T206, block every real-engine verify
  (L1:507); T227 to T229, the rest of L2-0a, wait on lane 1's tasks. T101 lands lane 1's contract before
  group B codes against it, and T344 lane 3's before group C.
- **Group A** depends on Phase 0.
  - T103 comes first in `lanes-index` (lane 1's Question 11 PR); T502, the
    detached bind, is phase 1's first guard PR (#347).
  - T301 waits for T107; T302 for T301; T303 can start at once; T304 waits
    for T107 and T121.
  - Lane 5's T507 writes `register read --for guard`'s client half after
    T113, under lane 1's review (R07).
- **Group B** codes beside group A, against `contracts/register-cli.md` and
  the fake `psql`. It lands after slices 1a and 1b and after T111 to T114
  (L1:531). Its spine, T412 → T413 → T414 → T415 → T417 → T418, starts
  when slices 1a and 1b land; T416 runs beside it, after T415 and T321.
- **Checkpoint A+B** depends on groups A and B.
- **Group C** starts on T415 and T344. Lane 4's T416, the renderer's
  fork, lands after T321 (L4:247; SK:827 superseded, lane 3, fix round B,
  2026-11-09). Within group C: T320 comes first; T321 to T323 run in order; T324 to T327 follow T323, and T326 and T327 also follow lane 4's T416, and T326 also lane 4's T420 and T421 (their Depends);
  T328 comes last, after Checkpoint A+B.
- **The service** (T511 to T513) is built beside groups A and B, not after
  group C (Brett's "4A"; R35). It is coded and tested against the fixture's
  configuration while Q6(c)'s refusal stands, and no service PR lands before
  the spec PR. T512 waits on lane 1's T102 (met through T507), T103 to
  T106, T111, T113, T115 and T117; T112 where AT-10's fresh write uses it;
  T107 to T110 through lane 2's template; lane 3's T302; and lane 5's T504,
  T505 and T507. It does not wait on T328 or on group B (L1:449).
- **The release:** T342 can start at any time, and T346 at once, before
  T119's check 1, which depends on it; T340 and T341 wait until each
  mechanism they describe exists; T343 waits for everything; T345 runs
  before the change is archived.
- **The release tag** waits for group C and the service (Brett's "4A").
- **Acts 3 and 4** are outside 007. They wait on the install, and act 4 also
  on slices 2a, 2b and 3, and on the service.

- **T403's and T408's `--decide` lines land after T502**, not after T509
  (R38, as amended, 6726831985 and 6726857112).

- **T426 lands after T425,** which adds R45's shared line (lane 4's decision;

- **T512's `status` lines** follow T117 (R44).

### Lane 4's order (L4:421–446)

- **Phase 1:** after the spec PR, T401 to T411 but T409 may land ahead of
  slices 1a and 1b (R23); T409 carries the mode across 1a's `exec`, so it
  lands after 1a. T401 comes first, then T402, T403 and T404, which use
  its verbs. T405 to T408 wait for no lane 4 task; T403 and T408 wait for
  lane 5's T502, and nothing waits for T509. T410 lands after T409, and
  T411 waits for no lane 4 task; with T401, the three land in `lanes-edit.sh`
  one at a time. Every phase 1 task lands
  after its lane 2 module (L2-N4; L2-N2 for T409).
- **Phase 2:** the spine T412 → T413 → T414 → T415 → T417 → T418, after 1a
  and 1b; T413 also after T409 and T410. T416 runs beside it after T415 and T321; lane 3's T325 waits
  for it, and since the follow-up T326 and T327 do too (their Depends). T419 after T409 and 1b; T427 after T410 and 1b; T420, and
  T424 → T425, after T415 (T424 also after T122); T421, T422 and T423 each
  after T418 (T423 also after T504 and T506); T426 after T409, T504 and T425; T429 to T432 after T410 and T417;
  T428 after T427 too; T433 and T434 after T417.
- **Gates every group B task carries here:** `SPEC-PR`, `SLICE-1A`,
  `SLICE-1B` and lane 1's T111 to T114 (L1:531). `STOP until T101` stays on
  the 12 tasks whose lines name it, T510 among them. It is met for coding since 2026-11-09:
  lane 4 confirmed draft 4.5's §3.1 to §3.5 for group B (6746695468), and
  lane 3 ruled (6746783992). Local branches may code; merging still waits
  for `SPEC-PR` and each task's other gates.
- **The verifies** (round B9): every lane 4 run names lane 2's tests, by lane 4's re-point (#165 6752694348), which lane 3 accepted (6752888006), with its 16 gates on 15 tasks. Where a run is left whole, the task's own lines say what it waits for: T403, T405, T406 and T408 share `the_other_callers_arms`, T418 and T427 a tail test, and T432 its `[write]` cases. The `[J3]` phrase is gone from lane 4's lines (lane 3, #165 6752888006, question 1). T405 and T406 name T275's read-mirror case on their lines and leave it out of their verifies, since it fails until T328, which waits for Checkpoint A+B (lane 3, #165 6754806910). Lane 4's B9 delta (#165 6754773693; lane 3, 6754806910) moves the tests of questions 7, 9 and 10 (6752888006) among T402, T412, T415, T417 to T421, T423 and T428, with four new gates: T245 and T247 on T415, T248 on T421 and T217 on T428.
- **Lane 5's gates:** T401's primary branch waits for T505; T412 for T502;
  T413 for T504; T414 for T505; T423 for T504 and T506; T425 and T426 for
  T504, and T425's `who --landing` test for T505 too; T403 and T408 for
  T502.

### Lane 5's order (L5:569–689, with section D and the rulings)

- **T501 lands after the spec PR and before T502** (R50; lane 3, fix round B, 2026-11-09). T502
  is phase 1's first guard PR.
- **T503** after T502. **T504** after T409, whose `REG_MODE` and `lanes_index_bin` it uses (R45; Q-L5-2),
  T105 and T113. **T505** after T101, T504, T113 to T115. **T506** after
  T505 and T114. **T507** after T502, T504, T505, T101 to T104, T113, T121,
  T302 and T304. **T508** after T504 and T507. **T509** after T504 to T508
  and lane 4's T402 to T408.
- **T510** (group B) after T101, T502, T507, lane 4's T409, T410, T412 to
  T415, T417, T418, T427 and T428, and lane 1's T112 and T114.
- **T511 to T513** (the service) after the spec PR; T512 after T511 and
  the lane 1, lane 3 and lane 5 tasks its line names; T513 after T508,
  T511 and T512. Lane 1 reviews U5 before each lands.

### Within each phase

- The tests come first, and fail.
- Then the implementation, then the checkpoint.
- Each task is committed on its own, with explicit pathspecs.
- A file's pull requests land one at a time under Rule 6's `HOLD`. Two
  `lanes-edit.sh` tasks built in separate worktrees are rebased at landing
  one at a time (lane 4's order).

### Parallel opportunities

- T101 ∥ T102, in the spec leg.
- T303 ∥ T301: they touch different files.
- T340 ∥ T342.
- Lane 4's T402 to T408 are `[P]` with each other, and so are T428 to T434,
  since each touches only its own file (L4:421–446).
- Across lanes, group A's blocks run in parallel by function, but no two
  tasks of one file are `[P]`.
